Proverbs 11:15	Hee shall be sore vexed, that is suretie for a stranger, and he that hateth suretiship, is sure.
Proverbs 11:16	A gracious woman atteineth honour, and the strong men atteine riches.

Proverbs 11:18	The wicked worketh a deceitful worke: but hee that soweth righteousnes, shall receiue a sure rewarde.
Proverbs 11:19	As righteousnes leadeth to life: so hee that followeth euill, seeketh his owne death.
Proverbs 11:20	They that are of a froward heart, are abomination to the Lord: but they that are vpright in their way, are his delite.
Proverbs 11:21	Though hande ioyne in hande, the wicked shall not be vnpunished: but the seede of the righteous shall escape.
Proverbs 11:22	As a iewell of golde in a swines snoute: so is a faire woman, which lacketh discretion.
Proverbs 11:23	The desire of the righteous is onely good: but the hope of the wicked is indignation.
Proverbs 11:24	There is that scattereth, and is more increased: but hee that spareth more then is right, surely commeth to pouertie.

Proverbs 11:26	He that withdraweth the corne, the people will curse him: but blessing shalbe vpon the head of him that selleth corne.
Proverbs 11:27	He that seeketh good things, getteth fauour: but he that seeketh euill, it shall come to him.

Proverbs 18:1	For the desire thereof hee will separate himselfe to seeke it, and occupie himselfe in all wisdome.
Proverbs 18:2	A foole hath no delite in vnderstanding: but that his heart may be discouered.

Proverbs 18:5	It is not good to accept the person of the wicked, to cause ye righteous to fall in iudgement.
Proverbs 18:6	A fooles lips come with strife, and his mouth calleth for stripes.
Proverbs 18:7	A fooles mouth is his owne destruction, and his lips are a snare for his soule.
Proverbs 18:8	The wordes of a tale bearer are as flatterings, and they goe downe into the bowels of the belly.
Proverbs 18:9	He also that is slouthfull in his worke, is euen the brother of him that is a great waster.

Proverbs 18:11	The rich mans riches are his strong citie: and as an hie wall in his imagination.

Proverbs 18:13	He that answereth a matter before hee heare it, it is folly and shame vnto him.

Proverbs 18:15	A wise heart getteth knowledge, and the eare of the wise seeketh learning.

Proverbs 18:18	The lot causeth contentions to cease, and maketh a partition among the mightie.
Proverbs 18:19	A brother offended is harder to winne then a strong citie, and their contentions are like the barre of a palace.
Proverbs 18:20	With the fruite of a mans mouth shall his belly be satisfied, and with the increase of his lips shall he be filled.
Proverbs 18:21	Death and life are in the power of ye tongue, and they that loue it, shall eate the fruite thereof.
Proverbs 18:22	He that findeth a wife, findeth a good thing, and receiueth fauour of the Lord.
Proverbs 18:23	The poore speaketh with prayers: but the rich answereth roughly.

Proverbs 19:1	Better is the poore that walketh in his vprightnes, then he that abuseth his lips, and is a foole.
Proverbs 19:2	For without knowledge the minde is not good, and he that hasteth with his feete, sinneth.

Proverbs 19:4	Riches gather many friends: but the poore is separated from his neighbour.
Proverbs 19:5	A false witnes shall not be vnpunished: and he that speaketh lyes, shall not escape.
Proverbs 19:6	Many reuerence the face of the prince, and euery man is friend to him that giueth giftes.
Proverbs 19:7	All the brethren of the poore doe hate him: howe much more will his friends depart farre from him? though hee be instant with wordes, yet they will not.
Proverbs 19:8	He that possesseth vnderstanding, loueth his owne soule, and keepeth wisdome to finde goodnesse.
Proverbs 19:9	A false witnes shall not be vnpunished: and he that speaketh lyes, shall perish.
Proverbs 19:10	Pleasure is not comely for a foole, much lesse for a seruant to haue rule ouer princes.
Proverbs 19:11	The discretion of man deferreth his anger: and his glory is to passe by an offence.

Proverbs 19:14	House and riches are the inheritance of the fathers: but a prudent wife commeth of the Lord.
Proverbs 19:15	Slouthfulnes causeth to fall asleepe, and a deceitfull person shall be affamished.

Proverbs 19:17	He that hath mercy vpon the poore, lendeth vnto the Lord: and the Lord will recompense him that which he hath giuen.
Proverbs 19:18	Chasten thy sonne while there is hope, and let not thy soule spare for his murmuring.

Proverbs 19:20	Heare counsell and receiue instruction, that thou mayest be wise in thy latter ende.
Proverbs 19:21	Many deuises are in a mans heart: but the counsell of the Lord shall stand.
Proverbs 19:22	That that is to be desired of a man, is his goodnes, and a poore man is better then a lyer.
Proverbs 19:23	The feare of the Lord leadeth to life: and he that is filled therewith, shall continue, and shall not be visited with euill.
Proverbs 19:24	The slouthfull hideth his hand in his bosome, and wil not put it to his mouth againe.

Proverbs 20:4	The slouthfull will not plowe, because of winter: therefore shall he beg in sommer, but haue nothing.

Proverbs 24:5	A wise man is strong: for a man of vnderstanding encreaseth his strength.
Proverbs 24:6	For with counsel thou shalt enterprise thy warre, and in the multitude of them that can giue counsell, is health.
Proverbs 24:7	Wisdome is hie to a foole: therefore he can not open his mouth in the gate.

Proverbs 24:26	They shall kisse the lippes of him that answereth vpright wordes.

Proverbs 25:1	THESE ARE ALSO PARABLES of Salomon, which the men of Hezekiah King of Iudah copied out.
Proverbs 25:2	The glorie of God is to conceale a thing secret: but the Kings honour is to search out a thing.
Proverbs 25:3	The heaues in height, and the earth in deepenes, and the Kings heart can no man search out.
Proverbs 25:4	Take the drosse from the siluer, and there shall proceede a vessell for the finer.
Proverbs 25:5	Take away the wicked from the King, and his throne shall be stablished in righteousnes.
Proverbs 25:6	Boast not thy selfe before the King, and stand not in the place of great men.
Proverbs 25:7	For it is better, that it be saide vnto thee, Come vp hither, then thou to be put lower in the presece of the prince whom thine eyes haue seene.
Proverbs 25:8	Goe not foorth hastily to strife, least thou know not what to doe in the ende thereof, when thy neighbour hath put thee to shame.
Proverbs 25:9	Debate thy matter with thy neighbour, and discouer not the secret to another,
Proverbs 25:10	Least he that heareth it put thee to shame, and thine infamie doe not cease.
Proverbs 25:11	A word spoken in his place, is like apples of golde with pictures of siluer.
Proverbs 25:12	He that reprooueth the wise, and the obedient eare, is as a golden earering and an ornament of fine golde.
Proverbs 25:13	As the colde of the snowe in the time of haruest, so is a faithfull messenger to them that send him: for he refresheth the soule of his masters.
Proverbs 25:14	A man that boasteth of false liberalitie, is like cloudes and winde without raine.
Proverbs 25:15	A Prince is pacified by staying of anger, and a soft tongue breaketh the bones.
Proverbs 25:16	If thou haue found hony, eate that is sufficient for thee, least thou be ouerfull, and vomit it.
Proverbs 25:17	Withdrawe thy foote from thy neighbours house, least he be weary of thee, and hate thee.
Proverbs 25:18	A man that beareth false witnes against his neighbour, is like an hammer and a sword, and a sharpe arrowe.
Proverbs 25:19	Confidence in an vnfaythfull man in time of trouble, is like a broken tooth and a sliding foote.
Proverbs 25:20	Hee that taketh away the garment in the colde season, is like vineger powred vpon nitre, or like him that singeth songs to an heauy heart.
Proverbs 25:21	If hee that hateth thee be hungry, giue him bread to eate, and if he be thirstie, giue him water to drinke.
Proverbs 25:22	For thou shalt lay coles vpon his head, and the Lord shall recompense thee.
Proverbs 25:23	As the Northwinde driueth away the raine, so doeth an angry countenance the slandering tongue.
Proverbs 25:24	It is better to dwell in a corner of the house top, then with a contentious woman in a wide house.
Proverbs 25:25	As are the colde waters to a weary soule, so is good newes from a farre countery.
Proverbs 25:26	A righteous man falling downe before the wicked, is like a troubled well, and a corrupt spring.
Proverbs 25:27	It is not good to eate much hony: so to search their owne glory is not glory.
Proverbs 25:28	A man that refraineth not his appetite, is like a citie which is broken downe and without walles.
Proverbs 26:1	As the snowe in the sommer, and as the raine in the haruest are not meete, so is honour vnseemely for a foole.
Proverbs 26:2	As the sparowe by flying, and the swallow by flying escape, so the curse that is causeles, shall not come.
Proverbs 26:3	Vnto the horse belongeth a whip, to the asse a bridle, and a rod to the fooles backe.
Proverbs 26:4	Answer not a foole according to his foolishnes, least thou also be like him.
Proverbs 26:5	Answere a foole according to his foolishnes, least he be wise in his owne conceite.
Proverbs 26:6	He that sendeth a message by the hand of a foole, is as he that cutteth off the feete, and drinketh iniquitie.
Proverbs 26:7	As they that lift vp the legs of the lame, so is a parable in a fooles mouth.
Proverbs 26:8	As the closing vp of a precious stone in an heape of stones, so is he that giueth glory to a foole.
Proverbs 26:9	As a thorne standing vp in the hand of a drunkard, so is a parable in the mouth of fooles.
Proverbs 26:10	The excellent that formed all things, both rewardeth the foole and rewardeth the transgressers.
Proverbs 26:11	As a dog turneth againe to his owne vomit, so a foole turneth to his foolishnes.
Proverbs 26:12	Seest thou a man wise in his owne conceite? more hope is of a foole then of him.
Proverbs 26:13	The slouthfull man sayth, A lyon is in the way: a lyon is in the streetes.
Proverbs 26:14	As the doore turneth vpon his hinges, so doeth the slouthfull man vpon his bed.
Proverbs 26:15	The slouthfull hideth his hand in his bosome, and it grieueth him to put it againe to his mouth.
Proverbs 26:16	The sluggard is wiser in his owne conceite, then seuen men that can render a reason.
Proverbs 26:17	He that passeth by and medleth with the strife that belongeth not vnto him, is as one that taketh a dog by the eares.
Proverbs 26:18	As he that faineth himselfe mad, casteth fire brands, arrowes, and mortall things,
Proverbs 26:19	So dealeth the deceitfull man with his friend and sayth, Am not I in sport?
Proverbs 26:20	Without wood the fire is quenched, and without a talebearer strife ceaseth.
Proverbs 26:21	As ye cole maketh burning coles, and wood a fire, so the contentious man is apt to kindle strife.
Proverbs 26:22	The wordes of a tale bearer are as flatterings, and they goe downe into the bowels of the belly.
Proverbs 26:23	As siluer drosse ouerlayde vpon a potsheard, so are burning lips, and an euill heart.
Proverbs 26:24	He that hateth, will counterfaite with his lips, but in his heart he layeth vp deceite.
Proverbs 26:25	Though he speake fauourably, beleeue him not: for there are seuen abominations in his heart.
Proverbs 26:26	Hatred may be couered by deceite: but the malice thereof shall be discouered in the congregation.
Proverbs 26:27	He that diggeth a pit shall fall therein, and he that rolleth a stone, it shall returne vnto him.
Proverbs 26:28	A false tongue hateth the afflicted, and a flattering mouth causeth ruine.
Proverbs 27:1	Boast not thy selfe of to morowe: for thou knowest not what a day may bring forth.
Proverbs 27:2	Let another man prayse thee, and not thine owne mouth: a stranger, and not thine owne lips.
Proverbs 27:3	A stone is heauie, and the sand weightie: but a fooles wrath is heauier then them both.
Proverbs 27:4	Anger is cruell, and wrath is raging: but who can stand before enuie?
Proverbs 27:5	Open rebuke is better then secret loue.
Proverbs 27:6	The wounds of a louer are faithful, and the kisses of an enemie are pleasant.
Proverbs 27:7	The person that is full, despiseth an hony combe: but vnto the hungry soule euery bitter thing is sweete.
Proverbs 27:8	As a bird that wandreth from her nest, so is a man that wandreth from his owne place.
Proverbs 27:9	As oyntment and perfume reioyce the heart, so doeth the sweetenes of a mans friend by hearty counsell.
Proverbs 27:10	Thine owne friend and thy fathers friend forsake thou not: neither enter into thy brothers house in the day of thy calamitie: for better is a neighbour that is neere, then a brother farre off.
Proverbs 27:11	My sonne, be wise, and reioyce mine heart, that I may answere him that reprocheth me.
Proverbs 27:12	A prudent man seeth the plague, and hideth himselfe: but the foolish goe on still, and are punished.
Proverbs 27:13	Take his garment that is surety for a stranger, and a pledge of him for the stranger.
Proverbs 27:14	He that prayseth his friend with a loude voyce, rising earely in the morning, it shall be counted to him as a curse.
Proverbs 27:15	A continual dropping in the day of raine, and a contentious woman are alike.
Proverbs 27:16	He that hideth her, hideth the winde, and she is as ye oyle in his right hand, that vttereth it selfe.
Proverbs 27:17	Yron sharpeneth yron, so doeth man sharpen the face of his friend.
Proverbs 27:18	He that keepeth the fig tree, shall eate the fruite thereof: so he that waiteth vpon his master, shall come to honour.
Proverbs 27:19	As in water face answereth to face, so the heart of man to man.
Proverbs 27:20	The graue and destruction can neuer be full, so the eyes of man can neuer be satisfied.
Proverbs 27:21	As is the fining pot for siluer and the fornace for golde, so is euery man according to his dignitie.
Proverbs 27:22	Though thou shouldest bray a foole in a morter among wheate brayed with a pestell, yet will not his foolishnes depart from him.
Proverbs 27:23	Be diligent to know ye state of thy flocke, and take heede to the heardes.
Proverbs 27:24	For riches remaine not alway, nor the crowne from generation to generation.
Proverbs 27:25	The hey discouereth it selfe, and the grasse appeareth, and the herbes of the mountaines are gathered.
Proverbs 27:26	The lambes are for thy clothing, and the goates are the price of the fielde.
Proverbs 27:27	And let the milke of the goates be sufficient for thy foode, for the foode of thy familie, and for the sustenance of thy maydes.
Proverbs 28:1	The wicked flee when none pursueth: but the righteous are bolde as a lyon.
Proverbs 28:2	For the transgression of the land there are many princes thereof: but by a man of vnderstanding and knowledge a realme likewise endureth long.
Proverbs 28:3	A poore man, if he oppresse the poore, is like a raging raine, that leaueth no foode.
Proverbs 28:4	They that forsake the Law, prayse the wicked: but they that keepe the Law, set themselues against them.
Proverbs 28:5	Wicked men vnderstand not iudgemnt: but they that seeke the Lord vnderstand all things.
Proverbs 28:6	Better is the poore that walketh in his vprightnesse, then hee that peruerteth his wayes, though he be riche.
Proverbs 28:7	He that keepeth the Law, is a childe of vnderstanding: but hee that feedeth the gluttons, shameth his father.
Proverbs 28:8	He that increaseth his riches by vsurie and interest, gathereth them for him that will be mercifull vnto the poore.
Proverbs 28:9	He that turneth away his eare from hearing the Law, euen his prayer shalbe abominable.
Proverbs 28:10	He that causeth the righteous to go astray by an euill way, shall fall into his owne pit, and the vpright shall inherite good things.
Proverbs 28:11	The riche man is wise in his owne conceite: but the poore that hath vnderstanding, can trie him.
Proverbs 28:12	When righteous men reioyce, there is great glory: but when the wicked come vp, the man is tried.
Proverbs 28:13	He that hideth his sinnes, shall not prosper: but he that confesseth, and forsaketh them, shall haue mercy.
Proverbs 28:14	Blessed is the man that feareth alway: but he that hardeneth his heart, shall fall into euill.
Proverbs 28:15	As a roaring lyon, and an hungry beare, so is a wicked ruler ouer the poore people.
Proverbs 28:16	A prince destitute of vnderstanding, is also a great oppressour: but hee that hateth couetousnes, shall prolong his dayes.
Proverbs 28:17	A man that doeth violence against the blood of a person, shall flee vnto the graue, and they shall not stay him.
Proverbs 28:18	He that walketh vprightly, shalbe saued: but he that is froward in his wayes, shall once fall.
Proverbs 28:19	He that tilleth his land, shall be satisfied with bread: but he that followeth the idle, shall be filled with pouertie.
Proverbs 28:20	A faythfull man shall abound in blessings, and he that maketh haste to be riche, shall not be innocent.
Proverbs 28:21	To haue respect of persons is not good: for that man will transgresse for a piece of bread.
Proverbs 28:22	A man with a wicked eye hasteth to riches, and knoweth not, that pouertie shall come vpon him.
Proverbs 28:23	He that rebuketh a man, shall finde more fauour at length, then he that flattereth with his tongue.
Proverbs 28:24	Hee that robbeth his father and mother, and sayth, It is no transgression, is the companion of a man that destroyeth.
Proverbs 28:25	He that is of a proude heart, stirreth vp strife: but he that trusteth in the Lord, shall be fatte.
Proverbs 28:26	Hee that trusteth in his owne heart, is a foole: but he that walketh in wisdome, shall be deliuered.
Proverbs 28:27	He that giueth vnto the poore, shall not lacke: but he that hideth his eyes, shall haue many curses.
Proverbs 28:28	When the wicked rise vp, men hide them selues: but when they perish, ye righteous increase.
Proverbs 29:1	A man that hardeneth his necke when he is rebuked, shall suddenly be destroyed and can not be cured.
Proverbs 29:2	When the righteous are in authoritie, the people reioyce: but when the wicked beareth rule, the people sigh.
Proverbs 29:3	A man that loueth wisdome, reioyceth his father: but he that feedeth harlots, wasteth his substance.
Proverbs 29:4	A King by iudgement mainteineth ye countrey: but a man receiuing giftes, destroyeth it.
Proverbs 29:5	A man that flattereth his neighbour, spreadeth a net for his steps.
Proverbs 29:6	In the transgression of an euill man is his snare: but the righteous doeth sing and reioyce.
Proverbs 29:7	The righteous knoweth the cause of the poore: but the wicked regardeth not knowledge.
Proverbs 29:8	Scornefull men bring a citie into a snare: but wise men turne away wrath.
Proverbs 29:9	If a wise man contend with a foolish man, whether he be angry or laugh, there is no rest.
Proverbs 29:10	Bloodie men hate him that is vpright: but the iust haue care of his soule.
Proverbs 29:11	A foole powreth out all his minde: but a wise man keepeth it in till afterward.
Proverbs 29:12	Of a prince that hearkeneth to lyes, all his seruants are wicked.
Proverbs 29:13	The poore and the vsurer meete together, and the Lord lighteneth both their eyes.
Proverbs 29:14	A King that iudgeth the poore in trueth, his throne shalbe established for euer.
Proverbs 29:15	The rodde and correction giue wisdome: but a childe set a libertie, maketh his mother ashamed.
Proverbs 29:16	When the wicked are increased, transgression increaseth: but ye righteous shall see their fall.
Proverbs 29:17	Correct thy sonne and he will giue thee rest, and will giue pleasures to thy soule.
Proverbs 29:18	Where there is no vision, the people decay: but he that keepeth the Lawe, is blessed.
Proverbs 29:19	A seruant will not be chastised with words: though he vnderstand, yet he will not answere.
Proverbs 29:20	Seest thou a man hastie in his matters? there is more hope of a foole, then of him.
Proverbs 29:21	He that delicately bringeth vp his seruant from youth, at length he will be euen as his sone.
Proverbs 29:22	An angrie man stirreth vp strife, and a furious man aboundeth in transgression.
Proverbs 29:23	The pride of a man shall bring him lowe: but the humble in spirit shall enioy glory.
Proverbs 29:24	He that is partner with a thiefe, hateth his owne soule: he heareth cursing, and declareth it not.
Proverbs 29:25	The feare of man bringeth a snare: but he that trusteth in the Lord, shalbe exalted.
Proverbs 29:26	Many doe seeke the face of the ruler: but euery mans iudgement commeth from the Lord.
Proverbs 29:27	A wicked man is abomination to the iust, and he that is vpright in his way, is abomination to the wicked.
Proverbs 30:1	THE WORDS OF AGUR THE SONNE OF JAKEH. The prophecie which ye man spake vnto Ithiel, euen to Ithiel, and Vcal.
Proverbs 30:2	Surely I am more foolish then any man, and haue not the vnderstanding of a man in me.
Proverbs 30:3	For I haue not learned wisedome, nor atteined to the knowledge of holy things.
Proverbs 30:4	Who hath ascended vp to heauen, and descended? Who hath gathered the winde in his fist? Who hath bound the waters in a garment? Who hath established all the endes of the world? What is his name, and what is his sonnes name, if thou canst tell?
Proverbs 30:5	Euery worde of God is pure: he is a shield to those, that trust in him.
Proverbs 30:6	Put nothing vnto his wordes, least he reproue thee, and thou be found a lyar.
Proverbs 30:7	Two things haue I required of thee: denie me them not before I die.
Proverbs 30:8	Remooue farre from me vanitie and lyes: giue me not pouertie, nor riches: feede me with foode conuenient for me,
Proverbs 30:9	Least I be full, and denie thee, and say, Who is the Lord? or least I be poore and steale, and take the Name of my God in vaine.
Proverbs 30:10	Accuse not a seruant vnto his master, lest he curse thee, when thou hast offended.
Proverbs 30:11	There is a generation that curseth their father, and doeth not blesse their mother.
Proverbs 30:12	There is a generation that are pure in their owne conceite, and yet are not washed from their filthinesse.
Proverbs 30:13	There is a generation, whose eies are hautie, and their eye liddes are lifted vp.
Proverbs 30:14	There is a generation, whose teeth are as swordes, and their chawes as kniues to eate vp the afflicted out of the earth, and the poore from among men.
Proverbs 30:15	The horse leache hath two daughters which crye, Giue, giue. There be three things that will not be satisfied: yea, foure that say not, It is ynough.
Proverbs 30:16	The graue, and the barren wombe, the earth that cannot be satisfied with water, and the fire that sayeth not, It is ynough.
Proverbs 30:17	The eye that mocketh his father and despiseth the instruction of his mother, let ye rauens of the valley picke it out, and the yong eagles eate it.
Proverbs 30:18	There be three thinges hid from me: yea, foure that I knowe not,
Proverbs 30:19	The way of an eagle in the aire, the way of a serpent vpon a stone, ye way of a ship in ye middes of the sea, and the way of a man with a maide.
Proverbs 30:20	Such is ye way also of an adulterous woman: she eateth and wipeth her mouth, and sayth, I haue not committed iniquitie.
Proverbs 30:21	For three things the earth is moued: yea, for foure it cannot susteine it selfe:
Proverbs 30:22	For a seruant when he reigneth, and a foole when he is filled with meate,
Proverbs 30:23	For the hatefull woman, when she is married, and for a handmaid that is heire to her mistres.
Proverbs 30:24	These be foure small things in the earth, yet they are wise and full of wisedome:
Proverbs 30:25	The pismires a people not strong, yet prepare they their meate in sommer:
Proverbs 30:26	The conies a people not mightie, yet make their houses in the rocke:
Proverbs 30:27	The grashopper hath no King, yet goe they forth all by bandes:
Proverbs 30:28	The spider taketh holde with her handes, and is in Kings palaces.
Proverbs 30:29	There be three thinges that order well their going: yea, foure are comely in going,
Proverbs 30:30	A lyon which is strong among beastes, and turneth not at the sight of any:
Proverbs 30:31	A lusty grayhound, and a goate, and a King against whom there is no rising vp.
Proverbs 30:32	If thou hast bene foolish in lifting thy selfe vp, and if thou hast thought wickedly, lay thine hand vpon thy mouth.
Proverbs 30:33	When one churneth milke, he bringeth foorth butter: and he that wringeth his nose, causeth blood to come out: so he that forceth wrath, bringeth foorth strife.
Proverbs 31:1	THE WORDS OF KING LEMUEL: The prophecie which his mother taught him.
Proverbs 31:2	What my sonne! and what ye sonne of my wombe! and what, O sonne of my desires!
Proverbs 31:3	Giue not thy strength vnto women, nor thy wayes, which is to destroy Kings.
Proverbs 31:4	It is not for Kings, O Lemuel, it is not for Kings to drink wine nor for princes strog drinke,
Proverbs 31:5	Lest he drinke and forget the decree, and change the iudgement of all the children of affliction.
Proverbs 31:6	Giue ye strong drinke vnto him that is readie to perish, and wine vnto them that haue griefe of heart.
Proverbs 31:7	Let him drinke, that he may forget his pouertie, and remember his miserie no more.
Proverbs 31:8	Open thy mouth for the domme in the cause of all the children of destruction.
Proverbs 31:9	Open thy mouth: iudge righteously, and iudge the afflicted, and the poore.
Proverbs 31:10	Who shall finde a vertuous woman? for her price is farre aboue the pearles.
Proverbs 31:11	The heart of her husband trusteth in her, and he shall haue no neede of spoyle.
Proverbs 31:12	She will doe him good, and not euill all the dayes of her life.
Proverbs 31:13	She seeketh wooll and flaxe, and laboureth cheerefully with her handes.
Proverbs 31:14	She is like the shippes of marchants: shee bringeth her foode from afarre.
Proverbs 31:15	And she riseth, whiles it is yet night: and giueth the portion to her houshold, and the ordinarie to her maides.
Proverbs 31:16	She considereth a field, and getteth it: and with the fruite of her handes she planteth a vineyarde.
Proverbs 31:17	She girdeth her loynes with strength, and strengtheneth her armes.
Proverbs 31:18	She feeleth that her marchandise is good: her candle is not put out by night.
Proverbs 31:19	She putteth her handes to the wherue, and her handes handle the spindle.
Proverbs 31:20	She stretcheth out her hand to the poore, and putteth foorth her hands to the needie.
Proverbs 31:21	She feareth not the snowe for her familie: for all her familie is clothed with skarlet.
Proverbs 31:22	She maketh her selfe carpets: fine linen and purple is her garment.
Proverbs 31:23	Her husband is knowen in the gates, when he sitteth with the Elders of the land.
Proverbs 31:24	She maketh sheetes, and selleth them, and giueth girdels vnto the marchant.
Proverbs 31:25	Strength and honour is her clothing, and in the latter day she shall reioyce.
Proverbs 31:26	She openeth her mouth with wisdome, and the lawe of grace is in her tongue.
Proverbs 31:27	She ouerseeth the wayes of her housholde, and eateth not the bread of ydlenes.
Proverbs 31:28	Her children rise vp, and call her blessed: her husband also shall prayse her, saying,
Proverbs 31:29	Many daughters haue done vertuously: but thou surmountest them all.
Proverbs 31:30	Fauour is deceitfull, and beautie is vanitie: but a woman that feareth the Lord, she shall be praysed.
Proverbs 31:31	Giue her of the fruite of her hands, and let her owne workes prayse her in the gates.

===== BOOK: Psalm =====
Psalm 1:1	Blessed is the man that doeth not walke in the counsell of the wicked, nor stand in the way of sinners, nor sit in ye seate of the scornefull:
Psalm 1:2	But his delite is in the Lawe of the Lord, and in his Lawe doeth he meditate day and night.
Psalm 1:3	For he shall be like a tree planted by the riuers of waters, that will bring foorth her fruite in due season: whose leafe shall not fade: so whatsoeuer he shall doe, shall prosper.
Psalm 1:4	The wicked are not so, but as the chaffe, which the winde driueth away.
Psalm 1:5	Therefore the wicked shall not stande in the iudgement, nor sinners in the assemblie of the righteous.
Psalm 1:6	For the Lord knoweth the way of the righteous, and the way of the wicked shall perish.
Psalm 2:1	Why doe the heathen rage, and the people murmure in vaine?
Psalm 2:2	The Kings of the earth band themselues, and the princes are assembled together against the Lord, and against his Christ.
Psalm 2:3	Let vs breake their bands, and cast their cordes from vs.
Psalm 2:4	But he that dwelleth in the heauen, shall laugh: the Lord shall haue them in derision.
Psalm 2:5	Then shall hee speake vnto them in his wrath, and vexe them in his sore displeasure, saying,
Psalm 2:6	Euen I haue set my King vpon Zion mine holy mountaine.
Psalm 2:7	I will declare the decree: that is, the Lord hath said vnto me, Thou art my Sonne: this day haue I begotten thee.
Psalm 2:8	Aske of me, and I shall giue thee the heathen for thine inheritance, and the endes of the earth for thy possession.
Psalm 2:9	Thou shalt krush them with a scepter of yron, and breake them in pieces like a potters vessell.
Psalm 2:10	Be wise nowe therefore, ye Kings: be learned ye Iudges of the earth.
Psalm 2:11	Serue the Lord in feare, and reioyce in trembling.
Psalm 2:12	Kisse the sonne, least he be angry, and ye perish in the way, when his wrath shall suddenly burne. blessed are all that trust in him.
Psalm 3:1	A Psalme of Dauid, when he fled from his sonne Absalom. Lord, howe are mine aduersaries increased? howe many rise against me?
Psalm 3:2	Many say to my soule, There is no helpe for him in God. Selah.
Psalm 3:3	But thou Lord art a buckler for me: my glory, and the lifter vp of mine head.
Psalm 3:4	I did call vnto the Lord with my voyce, and he heard me out of his holy mountaine. Selah.
Psalm 3:5	I layed me downe and slept, and rose vp againe: for the Lord susteined me.
Psalm 3:6	I will not be afrayde for ten thousand of the people, that should beset me round about.
Psalm 3:7	O Lord, arise: helpe me, my God: for thou hast smitten all mine enemies vpon the cheeke bone: thou hast broken the teeth of the wicked.
Psalm 3:8	Saluation belongeth vnto the Lord, and thy blessing is vpon thy people. Selah.
Psalm 4:1	To him that excelleth on Neginoth. A Psalme of Dauid. Heare me when I call, O God of my righteousnes: thou hast set me at libertie, when I was in distresse: haue mercie vpon me and hearken vnto my prayer.
Psalm 4:2	O ye sonnes of men, howe long will yee turne my glory into shame, louing vanitie, and seeking lyes? Selah.
Psalm 4:3	For be ye sure that the Lord hath chosen to himselfe a godly man: the Lord will heare when I call vnto him.
Psalm 4:4	Tremble, and sinne not: examine your owne heart vpon your bed, and be still. Selah.
Psalm 4:5	Offer the sacrifices of righteousnes, and trust in the Lord.
Psalm 4:6	Many say, Who will shewe vs any good? but Lord, lift vp the light of thy countenance vpon vs.
Psalm 4:7	Thou hast giuen mee more ioye of heart, then they haue had, when their wheate and their wine did abound.
Psalm 4:8	I will lay mee downe, and also sleepe in peace: for thou, Lord, onely makest me dwell in safetie.
Psalm 5:1	To him that excelleth upon Nehiloth. A Psalme of Dauid. Heare my wordes, O Lord: vnderstande my meditation.
Psalm 5:2	Hearken vnto the voyce of my crie, my King and my God: for vnto thee doe I pray.
Psalm 5:3	Heare my voyce in the morning, O Lord: for in the morning will I direct me vnto thee, and I will waite.
Psalm 5:4	For thou art not a God that loueth wickednes: neither shall euill dwell with thee.
Psalm 5:5	The foolish shall not stand in thy sight: for thou hatest all them that worke iniquitie.
Psalm 5:6	Thou shalt destroy them that speake lyes: the Lord will abhorre the bloodie man and deceitfull.
Psalm 5:7	But I wil come into thine house in the multitude of thy mercie: and in thy feare will I worship toward thine holy Temple.
Psalm 5:8	Leade me, O Lord, in thy righteousnes, because of mine enemies: make thy way plaine before my face.
Psalm 5:9	For no constancie is in their mouth: within, they are very corruption: their throte is an open sepulchre, and they flatter with their tongue.
Psalm 5:10	Destroy them, O God: let them fall from their counsels: cast them out for the multitude of their iniquities, because they haue rebelled against thee.
Psalm 5:11	And let all them that trust in thee, reioyce and triumph for euer, and couer thou them: and let them, that loue thy Name, reioyce in thee.
Psalm 5:12	For thou Lord wilt blesse the righteous, and with fauour wilt compasse him, as with a shielde.
Psalm 6:1	To him that excelleth on Neginoth upon the eith tune. A Psalme of Dauid. O lord, rebuke me not in thine anger, neither chastise me in thy wrath.
Psalm 6:2	Haue mercie vpon me, O Lord, for I am weake: O Lord heale me, for my bones are vexed.
Psalm 6:3	My soule is also sore troubled: but Lord how long wilt thou delay?
Psalm 6:4	Returne, O Lord: deliuer my soule: saue me for thy mercies sake.
Psalm 6:5	For in death there is no remembrance of thee: in the graue who shall prayse thee?
Psalm 6:6	I fainted in my mourning: I cause my bed euery night to swimme, and water my couch with my teares.
Psalm 6:7	Mine eye is dimmed for despight, and sunke in because of all mine enemies.
Psalm 6:8	Away from mee all ye workers of iniquitie: for the Lord hath heard the voyce of my weeping.
Psalm 6:9	The Lord hath heard my petition: the Lord will receiue my prayer.
Psalm 6:10	All mine enemies shall be confounded and sore vexed: they shall be turned backe, and put to shame suddenly.
Psalm 7:1	Shigaion of Dauid, which he sang unto the Lord, concerning the wordes of Chush the sonne of Iemini. O Lord my God, in thee I put my trust: saue me from all that persecute me, and deliuer me,
Psalm 7:2	Least he deuoure my soule like a lion, and teare it in pieces, while there is none to helpe.
Psalm 7:3	O Lord my God, if I haue done this thing, if there be any wickednes in mine handes,
Psalm 7:4	If I haue rewarded euill vnto him that had peace with mee, (yea I haue deliuered him that vexed me without cause)
Psalm 7:5	Then let the enemie persecute my soule and take it: yea, let him treade my life downe vpon the earth, and lay mine honour in the dust. Selah.
Psalm 7:6	Arise, O Lord, in thy wrath, and lift vp thy selfe against the rage of mine enemies, and awake for mee according to the iudgement that thou hast appointed.
Psalm 7:7	So shall the Congregation of the people compasse thee about: for their sakes therefore returne on hie.
Psalm 7:8	The Lord shall iudge the people: Iudge thou me, O Lord, according to my righteousnesse, and according to mine innocencie, that is in mee.
Psalm 7:9	Oh let the malice of the wicked come to an ende: but guide thou the iust: for the righteous God trieth the hearts and reines.
Psalm 7:10	My defence is in God, who preserueth the vpright in heart.
Psalm 7:11	God iudgeth the righteous, and him that contemneth God euery day.
Psalm 7:12	Except he turne, he hath whet his sword: he hath bent his bowe and made it readie.
Psalm 7:13	Hee hath also prepared him deadly weapons: hee will ordeine his arrowes for them that persecute me.
Psalm 7:14	Beholde, hee shall trauaile with wickednes: for he hath conceiued mischiefe, but he shall bring foorth a lye.
Psalm 7:15	Hee hath made a pitte and digged it, and is fallen into the pit that he made.
Psalm 7:16	His mischiefe shall returne vpon his owne head, and his crueltie shall fall vpon his owne pate.
Psalm 7:17	I wil praise the Lord according to his righteousnes, and will sing praise to the Name of the Lord most high.
Psalm 8:1	To him that excelleth on Gittith. A Psalme of Dauid. O Lord our Lord, how excellent is thy Name in all the worlde! which hast set thy glory aboue the heauens.
Psalm 8:2	Out of the mouth of babes and suckelings hast thou ordeined strength, because of thine enemies, that thou mightest still the enemie and the auenger.
Psalm 8:3	When I beholde thine heauens, euen the workes of thy fingers, the moone and the starres which thou hast ordeined,
Psalm 8:4	What is man, say I, that thou art mindefull of him? and the sonne of man, that thou visitest him?
Psalm 8:5	For thou hast made him a little lower then God, and crowned him with glory and worship.
Psalm 8:6	Thou hast made him to haue dominion in the workes of thine hands: thou hast put all things vnder his feete:
Psalm 8:7	All sheepe and oxen: yea, and the beastes of the fielde:
Psalm 8:8	The foules of the ayre, and the fish of the sea, and that which passeth through the paths of the seas.
Psalm 8:9	O Lord our Lord, howe excellent is thy Name in all the world!
Psalm 9:1	To him that excelleth vpon Muth Laben. A Psalme of Dauid. I will praise the Lord with my whole heart: I will speake of all thy marueilous workes.
Psalm 9:2	I will bee glad, and reioyce in thee: I will sing praise to thy Name, O most High,
Psalm 9:3	For that mine enemies are turned backe: they shall fall, and perish at thy presence.
Psalm 9:4	For thou hast maintained my right and my cause: thou art set in the throne, and iudgest right.
Psalm 9:5	Thou hast rebuked the heathen: thou hast destroyed the wicked: thou hast put out their name for euer and euer.
Psalm 9:6	O enemie, destructions are come to a perpetual end, and thou hast destroyed the cities: their memoriall is perished with them.
Psalm 9:7	But the Lord shall sit for euer: hee hath prepared his throne for iudgement.
Psalm 9:8	For he shall iudge the worlde in righteousnes, and shall iudge the people with equitie.
Psalm 9:9	The Lord also wil be a refuge for the poore, a refuge in due time, euen in affliction.
Psalm 9:10	And they that know thy Name, will trust in thee: for thou, Lord, hast not failed them that seeke thee.
Psalm 9:11	Sing praises to the Lord, which dwelleth in Zion: shewe the people his workes.
Psalm 9:12	For whe he maketh inquisition for blood, hee remembreth it, and forgetteth not the complaint of the poore.
Psalm 9:13	Haue mercie vpon mee, O Lord: consider my trouble which I suffer of them that hate mee, thou that liftest me vp from the gates of death,
Psalm 9:14	That I may shewe all thy praises within the gates of the daughter of Zion, and reioyce in thy saluation.
Psalm 9:15	The heathen are sunken downe in the pit that they made: in the nette that they hid, is their foote taken.
Psalm 9:16	The Lord is knowen by executing iudgement: the wicked is snared in the worke of his owne handes. Higgaion. Selah.
Psalm 9:17	The wicked shall turne into hell, and all nations that forget God.
Psalm 9:18	For the poore shall not bee alway forgotten: the hope of the afflicted shall not perish for euer.
Psalm 9:19	Vp Lord: let not man preuaile: let the heathen be iudged in thy sight.
Psalm 9:20	Put them in feare, O Lord, that the heathen may knowe that they are but men. Selah.
Psalm 10:1	Why standest thou farre off, O Lord, and hidest thee in due time, euen in affliction?
Psalm 10:2	The wicked with pride doeth persecute the poore: let them be taken in the craftes that they haue imagined.
Psalm 10:3	For the wicked hath made boast of his owne heartes desire, and the couetous blesseth himselfe: he contemneth the Lord.
Psalm 10:4	The wicked is so proude that hee seeketh not for God: hee thinketh alwayes, There is no God.
Psalm 10:5	His wayes alway prosper: thy iudgements are hie aboue his sight: therefore defieth he all his enemies.
Psalm 10:6	He saith in his heart, I shall neuer be moued, nor be in danger.
Psalm 10:7	His mouth is full of cursing and deceite and fraude: vnder his tongue is mischiefe and iniquitie.
Psalm 10:8	He lieth in waite in the villages: in the secret places doeth hee murder the innocent: his eyes are bent against the poore.
Psalm 10:9	He lyeth in waite secretly, euen as a lyon in his denne: he lyeth in waite to spoyle the poore: he doeth spoyle the poore, when he draweth him into his net.
Psalm 10:10	He croucheth and boweth: therefore heaps of the poore doe fall by his might.
Psalm 10:11	He hath said in his heart, God hath forgotten, he hideth away his face, and will neuer see.
Psalm 10:12	Arise, O Lord God: lift vp thine hande: forget not the poore.
Psalm 10:13	Wherefore doeth the wicked contemne God? he saith in his heart, Thou wilt not regard.
Psalm 10:14	Yet thou hast seene it: for thou beholdest mischiefe and wrong, that thou mayest take it into thine handes: the poore committeth himselfe vnto thee: for thou art the helper of the fatherlesse.
Psalm 10:15	Breake thou the arme of the wicked and malicious: searche his wickednes, and thou shalt finde none.
Psalm 10:16	The Lord is King for euer and euer: the heathen are destroyed foorth of his land.
Psalm 10:17	Lord, thou hast heard the desire of the poore: thou preparest their heart: thou bendest thine eare to them,
Psalm 10:18	To iudge the fatherlesse and poore, that earthly man cause to feare no more.
Psalm 11:1	To him that excelleth. A Psalme of Dauid. In the Lord put I my trust: howe say yee then to my soule, Flee to your mountaine as a birde?
Psalm 11:2	For loe, the wicked bende their bowe, and make readie their arrowes vpon the string, that they may secretly shoote at them, which are vpright in heart.
Psalm 11:3	For the foundations are cast downe: what hath the righteous done?
Psalm 11:4	The Lord is in his holy palace: the Lordes throne is in the heauen: his eyes wil consider: his eye lids will try the children of men.
Psalm 11:5	The Lord will try the righteous: but the wicked and him that loueth iniquitie, doeth his soule hate.
Psalm 11:6	Vpon the wicked he shall raine snares, fire, and brimstone, and stormie tempest: this is the porcion of their cup.
Psalm 11:7	For the righteous Lord loueth righteousnes: his countenance doeth beholde the iust.
Psalm 12:1	To him that excelleth upon the eight tune. A Psalme of Dauid. Helpe Lord, for there is not a godly man left: for the faithfull are fayled from among the children of men.
Psalm 12:2	They speake deceitfully euery one with his neighbour, flattering with their lips, and speake with a double heart.
Psalm 12:3	The Lord cut off all flattering lippes, and the tongue that speaketh proude things:
Psalm 12:4	Which haue saide, With our tongue will we preuaile: our lippes are our owne: who is Lord ouer vs?
Psalm 12:5	Now for the oppression of the needy, and for the sighes of the poore, I will vp, sayeth the Lord, and will set at libertie him, whom the wicked hath snared.
Psalm 12:6	The wordes of the Lord are pure wordes, as the siluer, tried in a fornace of earth, fined seuen folde.
Psalm 12:7	Thou wilt keepe them, O Lord: thou wilt preserue him from this generation for euer.
Psalm 12:8	The wicked walke on euery side: when they are exalted, it is a shame for the sonnes of men.
Psalm 13:1	To him that excelleth. A Psalme of Dauid. Howe long wilt thou forget me, O Lord, for euer? howe long wilt thou hide thy face from me?
Psalm 13:2	How long shall I take counsell within my selfe, hauing wearinesse dayly in mine heart? how long shall mine enemie be exalted aboue me?
Psalm 13:3	Beholde, and heare mee, O Lord my God: lighten mine eyes, that I sleepe not in death:
Psalm 13:4	Lest mine enemie say, I haue preuailed against him: and they that afflict me, reioyce when I slide.
Psalm 13:5	But I trust in thy mercie: mine heart shall reioyce in thy saluation: I will sing to the Lord, because he hath delt louingly with me.
Psalm 14:1	To him that excelleth. A Psalme of Dauid. The foole hath said in his heart, There is no God: they haue corrupted, and done an abominable worke: there is none that doeth good.
Psalm 14:2	The Lord looked downe from heauen vpon the children of men, to see if there were any that would vnderstand, and seeke God.
Psalm 14:3	All are gone out of the way: they are all corrupt: there is none that doeth good, no not one.
Psalm 14:4	Doe not all the workers of iniquitie know that they eate vp my people, as they eate bread? they call not vpon the Lord.
Psalm 14:5	There they shall be taken with feare, because God is in the generation of the iust.
Psalm 14:6	You haue made a mocke at the counsell of the poore, because the Lord is his trust.
Psalm 14:7	Oh giue saluation vnto Israel out of Zion: when the Lord turneth the captiuitie of his people, then Iaakob shall reioyce, and Israel shall be glad.
Psalm 15:1	A Psalme of Dauid. Lord, who shall dwell in thy Tabernacle? who shall rest in thine holy Mountaine?
Psalm 15:2	He that walketh vprightly and worketh righteousnes, and speaketh the trueth in his heart.
Psalm 15:3	He that slandereth not with his tongue, nor doeth euill to his neighbour, nor receiueth a false report against his neighbour.
Psalm 15:4	In whose eyes a vile person is contemned, but he honoureth them that feare the Lord: he that sweareth to his owne hinderance and changeth not.
Psalm 15:5	He that giueth not his money vnto vsurie, nor taketh reward against the innocent: hee that doeth these things, shall neuer be moued.
Psalm 16:1	Michtam of Dauid. Preserue mee, O God: for in thee doe I trust.
Psalm 16:2	O my soule, thou hast sayd vnto the Lord, Thou art my Lord: my weldoing extendeth not to thee,
Psalm 16:3	But to the Saints that are in the earth, and to the excellent: all my delite is in them.
Psalm 16:4	The sorowes of them, that offer to an other god, shall be multiplied: their offerings of blood will I not offer, neither make mention of their names with my lips.
Psalm 16:5	The Lord is the portion of mine inheritance and of my cup: thou shalt mainteine my lot.
Psalm 16:6	The lines are fallen vnto me in pleasant places: yea, I haue a faire heritage.
Psalm 16:7	I wil prayse the Lord, who hath giuen me counsell: my reines also teach me in the nightes.
Psalm 16:8	I haue set the Lord alwayes before me: for hee is at my right hand: therefore I shall not slide.
Psalm 16:9	Wherefore mine heart is glad and my tongue reioyceth: my flesh also doeth rest in hope.
Psalm 16:10	For thou wilt not leaue my soule in the graue: neither wilt thou suffer thine holy one to see corruption.
Psalm 16:11	Thou wilt shew me the path of life: in thy presence is the fulnesse of ioy: and at thy right hand there are pleasures for euermore.
Psalm 17:1	The prayer of Dauid. Heare the right, O Lord, consider my crye: hearken vnto my prayer of lips vnfained.
Psalm 17:2	Let my sentence come forth from thy presence, and let thine eyes beholde equitie.
Psalm 17:3	Thou hast prooued and visited mine heart in the night: thou hast tryed me, and foundest nothing: for I was purposed that my mouth should not offend.
Psalm 17:4	Concerning the workes of men, by the wordes of thy lips I kept mee from the paths of the cruell man.
Psalm 17:5	Stay my steps in thy paths, that my feete doe not slide.
Psalm 17:6	I haue called vpon thee: surely thou wilt heare me, O God: incline thine eare to me, and hearken vnto my wordes.
Psalm 17:7	Shewe thy marueilous mercies, thou that art the Sauiour of them that trust in thee, from such as resist thy right hand.
Psalm 17:8	Keepe me as the apple of the eye: hide me vnder the shadowe of thy wings,
Psalm 17:9	From the wicked that oppresse mee, from mine enemies, which compasse me round about for my soule.
Psalm 17:10	They are inclosed in their owne fat, and they haue spoken proudely with their mouth.
Psalm 17:11	They haue compassed vs now in our steps: they haue set their eyes to bring downe to the ground:
Psalm 17:12	Like as a lyon that is greedy of pray, and as it were a lyons whelp lurking in secret places.
Psalm 17:13	Vp Lord, disappoint him: cast him downe: deliuer my soule from the wicked with thy sworde,
Psalm 17:14	From men by thine hand, O Lord, from men of the world, who haue their portion in this life, whose bellies thou fillest with thine hid treasure: their children haue ynough, and leaue the rest of their substance for their children.
Psalm 17:15	But I will beholde thy face in righteousnes, and when I awake, I shalbe satisfied with thine image.
Psalm 18:1	To him that excelleth. A Psalme of Dauid the seruant of the Lord, which spake unto the Lord the wordes of this song (in the day that the Lord delivered him for the hande of all this enemies, and form the and of saul) and sayd, I will loue thee dearely, O Lord my strength.
Psalm 18:2	The Lord is my rocke, and my fortresse, and he that deliuereth me, my God and my strength: in him will I trust, my shield, the horne also of my saluation, and my refuge.
Psalm 18:3	I will call vpon the Lord, which is worthie to be praysed: so shall I be safe from mine enemies.
Psalm 18:4	The sorowes of death compassed me, and the floods of wickednes made me afraide.
Psalm 18:5	The sorowes of the graue haue compassed me about: the snares of death ouertooke me.
Psalm 18:6	But in my trouble did I call vpon the Lord, and cryed vnto my God: he heard my voyce out of his Temple, and my crye did come before him, euen into his eares.
Psalm 18:7	Then the earth trembled, and quaked: the foundations also of the mountaines mooued and shooke, because he was angrie.
Psalm 18:8	Smoke went out at his nostrels, and a consuming fire out of his mouth: coales were kindled thereat.
Psalm 18:9	He bowed the heauens also and came downe, and darkenes was vnder his feete.
Psalm 18:10	And he rode vpon Cherub and did flie, and he came flying vpon the wings of the winde.
Psalm 18:11	He made darkenes his secrete place, and his pauilion round about him, euen darkenesse of waters, and cloudes of the ayre.
Psalm 18:12	At the brightnes of his presence his clouds passed, haylestones and coles of fire.
Psalm 18:13	The Lord also thundred in the heauen, and the Highest gaue his voyce, haylestones and coales of fire.
Psalm 18:14	Then hee sent out his arrowes and scattred them, and he increased lightnings and destroyed them.
Psalm 18:15	And the chanels of waters were seene, and the foundations of the worlde were discouered at thy rebuking, O Lord, at the blasting of the breath of thy nostrels.
Psalm 18:16	He hath sent downe from aboue and taken mee: hee hath drawen mee out of many waters.
Psalm 18:17	He hath deliuered mee from my strong enemie, and from them which hate me: for they were too strong for me.
Psalm 18:18	They preuented me in the day of my calamitie: but the Lord was my stay.
Psalm 18:19	Hee brought mee foorth also into a large place: hee deliuered mee because hee fauoured me.
Psalm 18:20	The Lord rewarded me according to my righteousnes: according to the purenes of mine hands he recompensed me:
Psalm 18:21	Because I kept the wayes of the Lord, and did not wickedly against my God.
Psalm 18:22	For all his Lawes were before mee, and I did not cast away his commandements from mee.
Psalm 18:23	I was vpright also with him, and haue kept me from my wickednes.
Psalm 18:24	Therefore the Lord rewarded me according to my righteousnesse, and according to the purenes of mine hands in his sight.
Psalm 18:25	With the godly thou wilt shewe thy selfe godly: with the vpright man thou wilt shew thy selfe vpright.
Psalm 18:26	With the pure thou wilt shewe thy selfe pure, and with the froward thou wilt shewe thy selfe froward.
Psalm 18:27	Thus thou wilt saue the poore people, and wilt cast downe the proude lookes.
Psalm 18:28	Surely thou wilt light my candle: the Lord my God wil lighten my darkenes.
Psalm 18:29	For by thee I haue broken through an hoste, and by my God I haue leaped ouer a wall.
Psalm 18:30	The way of God is vncorrupt: the worde of the Lord is tried in the fire: he is a shield to all that trust in him.
Psalm 18:31	For who is God besides the Lord? and who is mightie saue our God?
Psalm 18:32	God girdeth me with strength, and maketh my way vpright.
Psalm 18:33	He maketh my feete like hindes feete, and setteth me vpon mine high places.
Psalm 18:34	He teacheth mine hands to fight: so that a bowe of brasse is broken with mine armes.
Psalm 18:35	Thou hast also giuen me the shield of thy saluation, and thy right hand hath stayed me, and thy louing kindenes hath caused me to increase.
Psalm 18:36	Thou hast enlarged my steps vnder mee, and mine heeles haue not slid.
Psalm 18:37	I haue pursued mine enemies, and taken them, and haue not turned againe till I had consumed them.
Psalm 18:38	I haue wounded them, that they were not able to rise: they are fallen vnder my feete.
Psalm 18:39	For thou hast girded me with strength to battell: them, that rose against me, thou hast subdued vnder me.
Psalm 18:40	And thou hast giuen me the neckes of mine enemies, that I might destroy them that hate me.
Psalm 18:41	They cryed but there was none to saue them, euen vnto the Lord, but hee answered them not.
Psalm 18:42	Then I did beate them small as the dust before the winde: I did treade them flat as the clay in the streetes.
Psalm 18:43	Thou hast deliuered me from the contentions of the people: thou hast made me the head of the heathen: a people, whom I haue not knowen, shall serue me.
Psalm 18:44	As soone as they heare, they shall obey me: the strangers shall be in subiection to me.
Psalm 18:45	Strangers shall shrinke away, and feare in their priuie chambers.
Psalm 18:46	Let the Lord liue, and blessed be my strength, and the God of my saluation be exalted.
Psalm 18:47	It is God that giueth me power to auenge me, and subdueth the people vnder me.
Psalm 18:48	O my deliuerer from mine enemies, euen thou hast set mee vp from them, that rose against me: thou hast deliuered mee from the cruell man.
Psalm 18:49	Therefore I will prayse thee, O Lord, among the nations, and wil sing vnto thy Name.
Psalm 18:50	Great deliuerances giueth hee vnto his King, and sheweth mercie to his anoynted, euen to Dauid, and to his seede for euer.
Psalm 19:1	To him that excelleth. A Psalme of Dauid. The heauens declare the glory of God, and the firmament sheweth ye worke of his hands.
Psalm 19:2	Day vnto day vttereth the same, and night vnto night teacheth knowledge.
Psalm 19:3	There is no speach nor language, where their voyce is not heard.
Psalm 19:4	Their line is gone forth through all the earth, and their words into the endes of the world: in them hath he set a tabernacle for the sunne.
Psalm 19:5	Which commeth forth as a bridegrome out of his chamber, and reioyceth like a mightie man to runne his race.
Psalm 19:6	His going out is from the ende of the heauen, and his compasse is vnto the endes of ye same, and none is hid from the heate thereof.
Psalm 19:7	The Lawe of the Lord is perfite, conuerting the soule: the testimonie of the Lord is sure, and giueth wisedome vnto the simple.
Psalm 19:8	The statutes of the Lord are right and reioyce the heart: the commandement of the Lord is pure, and giueth light vnto the eyes.
Psalm 19:9	The feare of the Lord is cleane, and indureth for euer: the iudgements of the Lord are trueth: they are righteous altogether,
Psalm 19:10	And more to be desired then golde, yea, then much fine golde: sweeter also then honie and the honie combe.
Psalm 19:11	Moreouer by them is thy seruant made circumspect, and in keeping of them there is great reward.
Psalm 19:12	Who can vnderstand his faultes? clense me from secret fautes.
Psalm 19:13	Keepe thy seruant also from presumptuous sinnes: let them not reigne ouer me: so shall I be vpright, and made cleane from much wickednes.
Psalm 19:14	Let the wordes of my mouth, and the meditation of mine heart be acceptable in thy sight, O Lord, my strength, and my redeemer.
Psalm 20:1	To him that excelleth. A Psalme of Dauid. The Lord heare thee in the day of trouble: the name of ye God of Iaakob defend thee:
Psalm 20:2	Send thee helpe from the Sanctuarie, and strengthen thee out of Zion.
Psalm 20:3	Let him remember all thine offerings, and turne thy burnt offerings into asshes. Selah:
Psalm 20:4	And graunt thee according to thine heart, and fulfill all thy purpose:
Psalm 20:5	That we may reioyce in thy saluation, and set vp the banner in the Name of our God, when the Lord shall performe all thy petitions.
Psalm 20:6	Now know I that the Lord will helpe his anointed, and will heare him from his Sanctuarie, by the mightie helpe of his right hand.
Psalm 20:7	Some trust in chariots, and some in horses: but we will remember the Name of ye Lord our God.
Psalm 20:8	They are brought downe and fallen, but we are risen, and stand vpright.
Psalm 20:9	Saue Lord: let the King heare vs in the day that we call.
Psalm 21:1	To him that excelleth. A Psalme of Dauid. The King shall reioyce in thy strength, O Lord: yea how greatly shall he reioyce in thy saluation!
Psalm 21:2	Thou hast giuen him his hearts desire, and hast not denyed him the request of his lips. Selah.
Psalm 21:3	For thou diddest preuent him with liberall blessings, and didest set a crowne of pure gold vpon his head.
Psalm 21:4	He asked life of thee, and thou gauest him a long life for euer and euer.
Psalm 21:5	His glory is great in thy saluation: dignitie and honour hast thou laid vpon him.
Psalm 21:6	For thou hast set him as blessings for euer: thou hast made him glad with the ioy of thy countenance.
Psalm 21:7	Because the King trusteth in the Lord, and in the mercie of the most High, he shall not slide.
Psalm 21:8	Thine hand shall finde out all thine enemies, and thy right hand shall finde out them that hate thee.
Psalm 21:9	Thou shalt make them like a fierie ouen in time of thine anger: the Lord shall destroy them in his wrath, and the fire shall deuoure them.
Psalm 21:10	Their fruite shalt thou destroy from the earth, and their seede from the children of men.
Psalm 21:11	For they intended euill against thee, and imagined mischiefe, but they shall not preuaile.
Psalm 21:12	Therefore shalt thou put them aparte, and the strings of thy bowe shalt thou make readie against their faces.
Psalm 21:13	Be thou exalted, O Lord, in thy strength: so will we sing and prayse thy power.
Psalm 22:1	To him that excelleth upon Aiieleth Hasshahar. A Psalme of Dauid. My God, my God, why hast thou forsaken me, and art so farre from mine health, and from the wordes of my roaring?
Psalm 22:2	O my God, I crie by day, but thou hearest not, and by night, but haue no audience.
Psalm 22:3	But thou art holy, and doest inhabite the prayses of Israel.
Psalm 22:4	Our fathers trusted in thee: they trusted, and thou didest deliuer them.
Psalm 22:5	They called vpon thee, and were deliuered: they trusted in thee, and were not confounded.
Psalm 22:6	But I am a worme, and not a man: a shame of men, and the contempt of the people.
Psalm 22:7	All they that see me, haue me in derision: they make a mowe and nod the head, saying,
Psalm 22:8	He trusted in the Lord, let him deliuer him: let him saue him, seeing he loueth him.
Psalm 22:9	But thou didest draw me out of ye wombe: thou gauest me hope, euen at my mothers breasts.
Psalm 22:10	I was cast vpon thee, euen from ye wombe: thou art my God from my mothers belly.
Psalm 22:11	Be not farre from me, because trouble is neere: for there is none to helpe me.
Psalm 22:12	Many yong bulles haue compassed me: mightie bulles of Bashan haue closed me about.
Psalm 22:13	They gape vpon me with their mouthes, as a ramping and roaring lyon.
Psalm 22:14	I am like water powred out, and all my bones are out of ioynt: mine heart is like waxe: it is molten in the middes of my bowels.
Psalm 22:15	My strength is dryed vp like a potsheard, and my tongue cleaueth to my iawes, and thou hast brought me into the dust of death.
Psalm 22:16	For dogges haue compassed me, and the assemblie of the wicked haue inclosed me: they perced mine hands and my feete.
Psalm 22:17	I may tell all my bones: yet they beholde, and looke vpon me.
Psalm 22:18	They part my garments among them, and cast lottes vpon my vesture.
Psalm 22:19	But be thou not farre off, O Lord, my strength: hasten to helpe me.
Psalm 22:20	Deliuer my soule from the sword: my desolate soule from the power of the dogge.
Psalm 22:21	Saue me from the lyons mouth, and answere me in sauing me from the hornes of the vnicornes.
Psalm 22:22	I wil declare thy Name vnto my brethren: in the middes of the Congregation will I praise thee, saying,
Psalm 22:23	Prayse the Lord, ye that feare him: magnifie ye him, all the seede of Iaakob, and feare ye him, all the seede of Israel.
Psalm 22:24	For he hath not despised nor abhorred ye affliction of the poore: neither hath he hid his face from him, but when he called vnto him, he heard.
Psalm 22:25	My prayse shalbe of thee in the great Congregation: my vowes will I perfourme before them that feare him.
Psalm 22:26	The poore shall eate and be satisfied: they that seeke after the Lord, shall prayse him: your heart shall liue for euer.
Psalm 22:27	All the endes of the worlde shall remember themselues, and turne to the Lord: and all the kinreds of the nations shall worship before thee.
Psalm 22:28	For the kingdome is the Lords, and he ruleth among the nations.
Psalm 22:29	All they that be fat in the earth, shall eate and worship: all they that go downe into the dust, shall bowe before him, euen he that cannot quicken his owne soule.
Psalm 22:30	Their seede shall serue him: it shalbe counted vnto the Lord for a generation.
Psalm 22:31	They shall come, and shall declare his righteousnesse vnto a people that shall be borne, because he hath done it.
Psalm 23:1	A Psalme of David. The Lord is my shepheard, I shall not want.
Psalm 23:2	He maketh me to rest in greene pasture, and leadeth me by the still waters.
Psalm 23:3	He restoreth my soule, and leadeth me in the paths of righteousnesse for his Names sake.
Psalm 23:4	Yea, though I should walke through the valley of the shadowe of death, I will feare no euill: for thou art with me: thy rod and thy staffe, they comfort me.
Psalm 23:5	Thou doest prepare a table before me in the sight of mine aduersaries: thou doest anoynt mine head with oyle, and my cuppe runneth ouer.
Psalm 23:6	Doubtlesse kindnesse and mercie shall follow me all the dayes of my life, and I shall remaine a long season in the house of the Lord.
Psalm 24:1	A Psalme of David. The earth is the Lordes, and all that therein is: the worlde and they that dwell therein.
Psalm 24:2	For he hath founded it vpon the seas: and established it vpon the floods.
Psalm 24:3	Who shall ascende into the mountaine of the Lord? and who shall stand in his holy place?
Psalm 24:4	Euen he that hath innocent handes, and a pure heart: which hath not lift vp his minde vnto vanitie, nor sworne deceitfully.
Psalm 24:5	He shall receiue a blessing from the Lord, and righteousnesse from the God of his saluation.
Psalm 24:6	This is the generation of them that seeke him, of them that seeke thy face, this is Iaakob. Selah.
Psalm 24:7	Lift vp your heads ye gates, and be ye lift vp ye euerlasting doores, and the King of glory shall come in.
Psalm 24:8	Who is this King of glorie? the Lord, strong and mightie, euen the Lord mightie in battell.
Psalm 24:9	Lift vp your heads, ye gates, and lift vp your selues, ye euerlasting doores, and the King of glorie shall come in.
Psalm 24:10	Who is this King of glory? the Lord of hostes, he is the King of glorie. Selah.
Psalm 25:1	A Psalme of David. Unto thee, O Lord, lift I vp my soule.
Psalm 25:2	My God, I trust in thee: let me not be confounded: let not mine enemies reioyce ouer mee.
Psalm 25:3	So all that hope in thee, shall not be ashamed: but let them be confounded, that transgresse without cause.
Psalm 25:4	Shew me thy waies, O Lord, and teache me thy paths.
Psalm 25:5	Leade me foorth in thy trueth, and teache me: for thou art the God of my saluation: in thee doe I trust all the day.
Psalm 25:6	Remember, O Lord, thy tender mercies, and thy louing kindnesse: for they haue beene for euer.
Psalm 25:7	Remember not the sinnes of my youth, nor my rebellions, but according to thy kindenesse remember thou me, euen for thy goodnesse sake, O Lord.
Psalm 25:8	Gracious and righteous is the Lord: therefore will he teache sinners in the way.
Psalm 25:9	Them that be meeke, will hee guide in iudgement, and teach the humble his way.
Psalm 25:10	All the pathes of the Lord are mercie and trueth vnto such as keepe his couenant and his testimonies.
Psalm 25:11	For thy Names sake, O Lord, be merciful vnto mine iniquitie, for it is great.
Psalm 25:12	What man is he that feareth the Lord? him wil he teache the way that hee shall chuse.
Psalm 25:13	His soule shall dwell at ease, and his seede shall inherite the land.
Psalm 25:14	The secrete of the Lord is reueiled to them, that feare him: and his couenant to giue them vnderstanding.
Psalm 25:15	Mine eyes are euer towarde the Lord: for he will bring my feete out of the net.
Psalm 25:16	Turne thy face vnto mee, and haue mercie vpon me: for I am desolate and poore.
Psalm 25:17	The sorowes of mine heart are enlarged: drawe me out of my troubles.
Psalm 25:18	Looke vpon mine affliction and my trauel, and forgiue all my sinnes.
Psalm 25:19	Beholde mine enemies, for they are manie, and they hate me with cruell hatred.
Psalm 25:20	Keepe my soule, and deliuer me: let me not be confounded, for I trust in thee.
Psalm 25:21	Let mine vprightnes and equitie preserue me: for mine hope is in thee.
Psalm 25:22	Deliuer Israel, O God, out of all his troubles.
Psalm 26:1	A Psalme of David. Judge me, O Lord, for I haue walked in mine innocency: my trust hath bene also in the Lord: therefore shall I not slide.
Psalm 26:2	Proue me, O Lord, and trie mee: examine my reines, and mine heart.
Psalm 26:3	For thy louing kindnesse is before mine eyes: therefore haue I walked in thy trueth.
Psalm 26:4	I haue not hanted with vaine persons, neither kept companie with the dissemblers.
Psalm 26:5	I haue hated the assemblie of the euill, and haue not companied with the wicked.
Psalm 26:6	I will wash mine handes in innocencie, O Lord, and compasse thine altar,
Psalm 26:7	That I may declare with the voyce of thankesgiuing, and set foorth all thy wonderous woorkes.
Psalm 26:8	O Lord, I haue loued the habitation of thine house, and the place where thine honour dwelleth.
Psalm 26:9	Gather not my soule with the sinners, nor my life with the bloodie men:
Psalm 26:10	In whose handes is wickednes, and their right hand is full of bribes.
Psalm 26:11	But I will walke in mine innocencie: redeeme me therefore, and be mercifull vnto me.
Psalm 26:12	My foote standeth in vprightnesse: I will praise thee, O Lord, in the Congregations.
Psalm 27:1	A Psalme of David. The Lord is my light and my saluation, whom shall I feare? the Lord is the strength of my life, of whome shall I be afraide?
Psalm 27:2	When the wicked, euen mine enemies and my foes came vpon mee to eate vp my flesh; they stumbled and fell.
Psalm 27:3	Though an hoste pitched against me, mine heart should not be afraide: though warre be raised against me, I will trust in this.
Psalm 27:4	One thing haue I desired of the Lord, that I will require, euen that I may dwell in the house of the Lord all the dayes of my life, to beholde the beautie of the Lord, and to visite his Temple.
Psalm 27:5	For in the time of trouble hee shall hide mee in his Tabernacle: in the secrete place of his pauillion shall he hide me, and set me vp vpon a rocke.
Psalm 27:6	And nowe shall hee lift vp mine head aboue mine enemies rounde about mee: therefore wil I offer in his Tabernacle sacrifices of ioy: I wil sing and praise the Lord.
Psalm 27:7	Hearken vnto my voyce, O Lord, when I crie: haue mercie also vpon mee and heare mee.
Psalm 27:8	When thou saidest, Seeke ye my face, mine heart answered vnto thee, O Lord, I will seeke thy face.
Psalm 27:9	Hide not therefore thy face from mee, nor cast thy seruat away in displeasure: thou hast bene my succour: leaue me not, neither forsake mee, O God of my saluation.
Psalm 27:10	Though my father and my mother shoulde forsake me, yet the Lord will gather me vp.
Psalm 27:11	Teache mee thy way, O Lord, and leade me in a right path, because of mine enemies.
Psalm 27:12	Giue me not vnto the lust of mine aduersaries: for there are false witnesses risen vp against me, and such as speake cruelly.
Psalm 27:13	I should haue fainted, except I had beleeued to see the goodnes of the Lord in the land of the liuing.
Psalm 27:14	Hope in the Lord: be strong, and he shall comfort thine heart, and trust in the Lord.
Psalm 28:1	A Psalme of David. Unto thee, O Lord, doe I crie: O my strength, be not deafe toward mee, lest, if thou answere me not, I be like them that goe downe into the pit.
Psalm 28:2	Heare the voyce of my petitions, when I crie vnto thee, when I holde vp mine handes towarde thine holy Oracle.
Psalm 28:3	Drawe mee not away with the wicked, and with the woorkers of iniquitie: which speake friendly to their neighbours, when malice is in their hearts.
Psalm 28:4	Reward them according to their deedes, and according to the wickednes of their inuentions: recompense them after the woorke of their handes: render them their reward.
Psalm 28:5	For they regarde not the woorkes of the Lord, nor the operation of his handes: therefore breake them downe, and builde them not vp.
Psalm 28:6	Praised be the Lord, for he hath heard the voyce of my petitions.
Psalm 28:7	The Lord is my strength and my shielde: mine heart trusted in him, and I was helped: therfore mine heart shall reioyce, and with my song will I praise him.
Psalm 28:8	The Lord is their strength, and he is the strength of the deliuerances of his anointed.
Psalm 28:9	Saue thy people, and blesse thine inheritance: feede them also, and exalt them for euer.
Psalm 29:1	A Psalme of David. Give vnto the Lord, ye sonnes of the mightie: giue vnto the Lord glorie and strength.
Psalm 29:2	Giue vnto the Lord glorie due vnto his Name: worship the Lord in the glorious Sanctuarie.
Psalm 29:3	The voyce of the Lord is vpon the waters: the God of glorie maketh it to thunder: the Lord is vpon the great waters.
Psalm 29:4	The voyce of the Lord is mightie: the voyce of the Lord is glorious.
Psalm 29:5	The voyce of the Lord breaketh the cedars: yea, the Lord breaketh the cedars of Lebanon.
Psalm 29:6	He maketh them also to leape like a calfe: Lebanon also and Shirion like a yong vnicorne.
Psalm 29:7	The voice of the Lord deuideth the flames of sire.
Psalm 29:8	The voice of the Lord maketh the wildernes to tremble: the Lord maketh the wildernes of Kadesh to tremble.
Psalm 29:9	The voice of the Lord maketh the hindes to calue, and discouereth the forests: therefore in his Temple doth euery man speake of his glory.
Psalm 29:10	The Lord sitteth vpon the flood, and the Lord doeth remaine King for euer.
Psalm 29:11	The Lord shall giue strength vnto his people: the Lord shall blesse his people with peace.
Psalm 30:1	A Psalme or song of the dedication of the house of David. I will magnifie thee, O Lord: for thou hast exalted mee, and hast not made my foe to reioyce ouer me.
Psalm 30:2	O Lord my God, I cried vnto thee, and thou hast restored me.
Psalm 30:3	O Lord, thou hast brought vp my soule out of the graue: thou hast reuiued me from them that goe downe into the pit.
Psalm 30:4	Sing praises vnto the Lord, ye his Saintes, and giue thankes before the remembrance of his Holinesse.
Psalm 30:5	For he endureth but a while in his anger: but in his fauour is life: weeping may abide at euening, but ioy commeth in the morning.
Psalm 30:6	And in my prosperitie I sayde, I shall neuer be moued.
Psalm 30:7	For thou Lord of thy goodnes hadest made my mountaine to stande strong: but thou didest hide thy face, and I was troubled.
Psalm 30:8	Then cried I vnto thee, O Lord, and praied to my Lord.
Psalm 30:9	What profite is there in my blood, when I go downe to the pit? shall the dust giue thankes vnto thee? or shall it declare thy trueth?
Psalm 30:10	Heare, O Lord, and haue mercy vpon me: Lord, be thou mine helper.
Psalm 30:11	Thou hast turned my mourning into ioy: thou hast loosed my sacke and girded mee with gladnesse.
Psalm 30:12	Therefore shall my tongue praise thee and not cease: O Lord my God, I will giue thankes vnto thee for euer.
Psalm 31:1	To him that excelleth. A Psalme of David. In thee, O Lord, haue I put my trust: let mee neuer be confounded: deliuer me in thy righteousnesse.
Psalm 31:2	Bowe downe thine eare to me: make haste to deliuer mee: be vnto me a stronge rocke, and an house of defence to saue me.
Psalm 31:3	For thou art my rocke and my fortresse: therefore for thy Names sake direct mee and guide me.
Psalm 31:4	Drawe mee out of the nette, that they haue layde priuilie for mee: for thou art my strength.
Psalm 31:5	Into thine hand I commend my spirit: for thou hast redeemed me, O Lord God of trueth.
Psalm 31:6	I haue hated them that giue them selues to deceitfull vanities: for I trust in the Lord.
Psalm 31:7	I wil be glad and reioyce in thy mercie: for thou hast seene my trouble: thou hast knowen my soule in aduersities,
Psalm 31:8	And thou hast not shut me vp in the hand of the enemie, but hast set my feete at large.
Psalm 31:9	Haue mercie vpon mee, O Lord: for I am in trouble: mine eye, my soule and my bellie are consumed with griefe.
Psalm 31:10	For my life is wasted with heauinesse, and my yeeres with mourning: my strength faileth for my paine, and my bones are consumed.
Psalm 31:11	I was a reproch among all mine enemies, but specially among my neighbours: and a feare to mine acquaintance, who seeing me in the streete, fled from me.
Psalm 31:12	I am forgotten, as a dead man out of minde: I am like a broken vessell.
Psalm 31:13	For I haue heard the rayling of great men: feare was on euery side, while they conspired together against mee, and consulted to take my life.
Psalm 31:14	But I trusted in thee, O Lord: I said, Thou art my God.
Psalm 31:15	My times are in thine hande: deliuer mee from the hande of mine enemies, and from them that persecute me.
Psalm 31:16	Make thy face to shine vpon thy seruant, and saue me through thy mercie.
Psalm 31:17	Let me not be confounded, O Lord: for I haue called vpon thee: let the wicked bee put to confusion, and to silence in the graue.
Psalm 31:18	Let the lying lips be made dumme, which cruelly, proudly and spitefully speake against the righteous.
Psalm 31:19	Howe great is thy goodnesse, which thou hast layde vp for them, that feare thee! and done to them, that trust in thee, euen before the sonnes of men!
Psalm 31:20	Thou doest hide them priuily in thy presence from the pride of men: thou keepest them secretly in thy Tabernacle from the strife of tongues.
Psalm 31:21	Blessed be the Lord: for hee hath shewed his marueilous kindenesse toward me in a strong citie.
Psalm 31:22	Though I said in mine haste, I am cast out of thy sight, yet thou heardest the voyce of my prayer, when I cryed vnto thee.
Psalm 31:23	Loue ye the Lord all his Saintes: for the Lord preserueth the faithfull, and rewardeth abundantly the proud doer.
Psalm 31:24	All ye that trust in the Lord, be strong, and he shall establish your heart.
Psalm 32:1	A Psalme of David to give instruction. Blessed is he whose wickednes is forgiuen, and whose sinne is couered.
Psalm 32:2	Blessed is the man, vnto whom the Lord imputeth not iniquitie, and in whose spirite there is no guile.
Psalm 32:3	When I helde my tongue, my bones consumed, or when I roared all the day,
Psalm 32:4	(For thine hand is heauie vpon me, day and night: and my moysture is turned into ye drought of summer. Selah)
Psalm 32:5	Then I acknowledged my sinne vnto thee, neither hid I mine iniquitie: for I thought, I will confesse against my selfe my wickednesse vnto the Lord, and thou forgauest the punishment of my sinne. Selah.
Psalm 32:6	Therefore shall euery one, that is godly, make his prayer vnto thee in a time, when thou mayest be founde: surely in the flood of great waters they shall not come neere him.
Psalm 32:7	Thou art my secret place: thou preseruest me from trouble: thou compassest me about with ioyfull deliuerance. Selah.
Psalm 32:8	I will instruct thee, and teache thee in the way that thou shalt goe, and I will guide thee with mine eye.
Psalm 32:9	Be ye not like an horse, or like a mule, which vnderstand not: whose mouthes thou doest binde with bit and bridle, least they come neere thee.
Psalm 32:10	Many sorowes shall come to the wicked: but he, that trusteth in the Lord, mercie shall compasse him.
Psalm 32:11	Be glad ye righteous, and reioyce in the Lord, and be ioyfull all ye, that are vpright in heart.
Psalm 33:1	Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous: for it becommeth vpright men to be thankefull.
Psalm 33:2	Prayse the Lord with harpe: sing vnto him with viole and instrument of ten strings.
Psalm 33:3	Sing vnto him a newe song: sing cheerefully with a loude voyce.
Psalm 33:4	For the word of the Lord is righteous, and all his workes are faithfull.
Psalm 33:5	He loueth righteousnesse and iudgement: the earth is full of the goodnesse of the Lord.
Psalm 33:6	By the worde of the Lord were the heauens made, and all the hoste of them by the breath of his mouth.
Psalm 33:7	He gathereth the waters of the sea together as vpon an heape, and layeth vp the depths in his treasures.
Psalm 33:8	Let all the earth feare the Lord: let al them that dwell in the world, feare him.
Psalm 33:9	For he spake, and it was done: he commanded, and it stood.
Psalm 33:10	The Lord breaketh the counsell of the heathen, and bringeth to nought the deuices of the people.
Psalm 33:11	The counsell of the Lord shall stand for euer, and the thoughts of his heart throughout all ages.
Psalm 33:12	Blessed is that nation, whose God is the Lord: euen the people that he hath chosen for his inheritance.
Psalm 33:13	The Lord looketh downe from heauen, and beholdeth all the children of men.
Psalm 33:14	From the habitation of his dwelling he beholdeth all them that dwell in the earth.
Psalm 33:15	He facioneth their hearts euery one, and vnderstandeth all their workes.
Psalm 33:16	The King is not saued by the multitude of an hoste, neither is the mightie man deliuered by great strength.
Psalm 33:17	A horse is a vaine helpe, and shall not deliuer any by his great strength.
Psalm 33:18	Beholde, the eye of the Lord is vpon them that feare him, and vpon them, that trust in his mercie,
Psalm 33:19	To deliuer their soules from death, and to preserue them in famine.
Psalm 33:20	Our soule waiteth for the Lord: for he is our helpe and our shielde.
Psalm 33:21	Surely our heart shall reioyce in him, because we trusted in his holy Name.
Psalm 33:22	Let thy mercie, O Lord, be vpon vs, as we trust in thee.
Psalm 34:1	A Psalme of Dauid, when he changed his behauiour before Abimelech, who droue him away, and he departed. I will alway giue thankes vnto the Lord: his praise shalbe in my mouth continually.
Psalm 34:2	My soule shall glory in the Lord: the humble shall heare it, and be glad.
Psalm 34:3	Praise ye the Lord with me, and let vs magnifie his Name together.
Psalm 34:4	I sought the Lord, and he heard me: yea, he deliuered me out of all my feare.
Psalm 34:5	They shall looke vnto him, and runne to him: and their faces shall not be ashamed, saying,
Psalm 34:6	This poore man cryed, and the Lord heard him, and saued him out of all his troubles.
Psalm 34:7	The Angel of the Lord pitcheth round about them, that feare him, and deliuereth them.
Psalm 34:8	Taste ye and see, howe gratious the Lord is: blessed is the man that trusteth in him.
Psalm 34:9	Feare the Lord, ye his Saintes: for nothing wanteth to them that feare him.
Psalm 34:10	The lyons doe lacke and suffer hunger, but they, which seeke the Lord, shall want nothing that is good.
Psalm 34:11	Come children, hearken vnto me: I will teache you the feare of the Lord.
Psalm 34:12	What man is he, that desireth life, and loueth long dayes for to see good?
Psalm 34:13	Keepe thy tongue from euill, and thy lips, that they speake no guile.
Psalm 34:14	Eschewe euill and doe good: seeke peace and follow after it.
Psalm 34:15	The eyes of the Lord are vpon the righteous, and his eares are open vnto their crie.
Psalm 34:16	But the face of the Lord is against them that doe euill, to cut off their remembrance from the earth.
Psalm 34:17	The righteous crie, and the Lord heareth them, and deliuereth them out of all their troubles.
Psalm 34:18	The Lord is neere vnto them that are of a contrite heart, and will saue such as be afflicted in Spirite.
Psalm 34:19	Great are the troubles of the righteous: but the Lord deliuereth him out of them all.
Psalm 34:20	He keepeth all his bones: not one of them is broken.
